Hybrid real-person newspaper photomontage with biting anatomical caricature: Exactly three East Asian adults fill the image - one woman and two men - with no other human figures. Ms. Yuki, thirty-three, Japanese, reclines backward across the white cube pedestal as the spatial center, her aubergine gallery coat fallen completely off both shoulders and splayed beneath her as an improvised cushion, celadon lining exposed to the flat halftone light, both breasts and torso fully visible; her pearl asymmetric skirt is twisted and risen to her hip line, exposing navel and the crest of her widened hips which spread transversely to three times ordinary scale, stopping cleanly at ordinary waist and mid-thigh with no adjacent compensation. Her face carries the same two alterations: cheeks puffed transversely to mallet width above a mouth reduced vertically to thread-thin line of discernment, now directed upward toward an unseen archway. Her arms extend backward and grip the pedestal's rear edge behind her head, arching her spine so her exaggerated pelvic girth presses prominently against the cube's front rim. To her left, Mr. Liu, twenty-nine, mainland Chinese, in rust registrar's jacket now hanging off one arm and sand straight-leg trousers, kneels at the pedestal's left base corner; his neck elongated longitudinally to two and a half times ordinary scale thrusts his ordinary face forward at her profile level, but now his elongated cervical column bends slightly so his chin nearly touches her shoulder, both his hands planted flat on the pedestal's left face as if steadying the structure itself rather than adjusting her pose. To her right, Mr. Sato, thirty-one, South Korean, in ink installation technician's coveralls with chartreuse tool loops catching the flat ink spot-color and blush cropped pants, stands at the pedestal's right shadow; his single facial deformation of eyes recessed to shadowed depths uniformly one-half ordinary aperture directs those dark pits toward her torso, while both his hands now grip her right knee, pulling her bent leg slightly toward him so her foot lifts from the cube surface. The white cube pedestal occupies thirty-five percent of the canvas, its flat matte planes converting shallow poster space into singular elevated focus.
